## Open uncertainties

1. ⚠️ **MacIntyre's life dates.** The chapter prints **(1929- )**. Verified against current sources, the standard form is now **(1929-2025)**. The chapter's form is reproduced where the chapter is quoted and corrected in this session's own prose, with the divergence stated at Lessons 13 and 20.
2. ⚠️ **Taylor and Sandel.** The chapter's open forms **(1931- )** and **(1953- )** were checked and stand.
3. ⚠️ **Taylor's work.** The chapter cites ***Philosophical Papers*** (1985). The canonical owner's thinker material instead rests on *Sources of the Self* (1989) and "The Politics of Recognition" (1994). Both are carried, separately marked, and neither is attributed to the other. ❌ The chapter's citation is **not** silently replaced.
4. ⚠️ **The Exponents row.** It names **Bentham**, **J.S. Mill**, **Laski**, **Robert Dahl**, **Lenin**, **Gramsci** and **Mao** — and the chapter gives none of them a date, an argument or a sentence of discussion. ⚠️ **Laski** is the one partial exception: he is named at p.94 as the author of **A Grammar of Politics and An Introduction to Politics**, cited only to illustrate the disciplinary sense of the word. The row is reproduced because it is part of the table; **no position is attributed to any of the seven** on its authority.
5. ⚠️ **Rousseau and Green.** They appear in the chapter under **early indications of this view** and again in the table's Exponents row for communitarianism. The canonical owner requires that they be treated as **precursors/anticipations**, not as communitarians. This file follows the owner, and the divergence is flagged at Lessons 13 and 23.
6. ⚠️ **Authority against force.** The chapter makes a policy authoritative when obeyed **purely from fear of consequences** (p.99) while also holding that **authority does not signify the use of brute force** (p.97). The chapter never draws the line between them. No reconstruction is presented as the chapter's.
7. ⚠️ **Easton against the Marxist thesis.** Easton's **An authoritative allocation of some values is unavoidable** (p.97) and the Marxist **the end of class-conflict marks an end of politics itself** (p.104) cannot both be true as stated. The chapter never stages the collision; the staging at Lesson 17 and Map 3 is this session's and is marked ⚠️.
8. ⚠️ **The illusion of consent.** As the chapter states it, no observed agreement can count against the suppression thesis, because agreement can always be redescribed as **an illusion of consent** (p.103). The chapter supplies no criterion separating genuine from illusory consent. The objection is raised as a reasoning point, not as a refutation attributed to any author.
9. ⚠️ **MacIntyre's symptom claim.** **The prevalence of crime and violence, the breakdown of the family, and the widespread drug abuse** in **some modern liberal states** (p.105) is given with no state named, no study, no period and no data, and its causal attribution is unargued. It is reproduced as the chapter's report of a **claim**, never as evidence. 📰 It is also a **2009** assertion.
10. 📰 **The USSR and China.** **The former USSR (1917)** and **the People's Republic of China (1949)** (p.106) are illustrations as the 2009 edition states them; the chapter draws from them only the finding that a classless society is **extremely difficult to realize**. No current political or economic claim about either state is made anywhere in this file.
11. ⚠️ **The chapter's Indian examples.** The reserved-seats and taxation illustrations (p.99) are given with no article, statute, rate or date, and they belong to a **2009** text. They are used to show how **authoritative allocation** works, never as a statement of current Indian law or policy. ➡️ Polity.
12. ⚠️ **Benn's sentence.** The chapter prints **the art of looking for trouble, finding it where it exists or not, diagnosing it wrongly, and applying the wrong remedy**, which differs in wording from the version circulating in other collections. The chapter's form is used when Gauba is cited; no attempt is made to adjudicate the original.
13. ⚠️ **The 2025 and 2026 search pass.** A search over reporting of the 2025 and 2026 GS Main papers returned no stem on the nature of politics or communitarianism. The absence is recorded as a **search result**, not as a verified fact about the papers; no GS PYQ is claimed for this topic.
14. ❌ **Political Theory is not a named GS paper owner.** It is supplementary conceptual support. No GS PYQ has been invented, and no synthetic route has been built to manufacture coverage. The nine Philosophy Optional stems below are carried as **separate, explicitly labelled application anchors** and are routed in the ledgers to other owners.
15. ⚠️ **No post-2009 substantive fact is introduced.** The only departures from the 2009 text are the verified bibliographic and life-date corrections listed at items 1-3, and the exam years of the Philosophy Optional papers. ❌ No post-2009 statistic, statute, scheme, report, judgment or policy appears anywhere in this file.

## Subject boundary

- ➡️ **Topic 04, Marxism and Neo-Marxism** owns historical and dialectical materialism, base and superstructure, mode of production as a developed concept, surplus value, hegemony and the neo-Marxist tradition. This chapter names the **mode of production** once and develops none of it; Lessons 16-17 stop where the chapter stops.
- ➡️ **The Aristotle owners** hold the *polis*, *telos*, the classification of constitutions and virtue theory. This chapter gives Aristotle one analogy and one conclusion, and no date or work.
- ➡️ **The justice owner** holds Rawls. ❌ Rawls is **not named anywhere** in this chapter; everything about the original position and the veil of ignorance at Lesson 23 and register §18 is 🔶 owner-extension material.
- ➡️ **The power and authority owner** holds Weber's typology and the faces-of-power debate. This chapter uses **authority** only as Easton defines it.
- ➡️ **The approaches owner** holds systems analysis as a method. This chapter supplies the **system / sub-system** vocabulary and nothing more.
- ➡️ **Humanism, Secularism and Multiculturalism** owns multiculturalism as a doctrine and the recognition literature, and it is the ledger-verified primary owner of **two** of the nine anchors — **2026 Q2(c)** and **2022 Q3(c)**.
- ➡️ **Individual and State** owns rights-and-obligation theory and is the ledger-verified primary owner of **six** of the nine — **2025 Q4(c)**, **2024 Q3(a)**, **2023 Q2(b)**, **2021 Q4(c)**, **2020 Q2(c)** and **2019 Q2(a)**.
- ➡️ **Forms of Government** owns liberal democracy as a form of government and is the ledger-verified primary owner of the remaining one — **2018 Q1(a)**.
- ❌ **Social and Political Ideals owns none of these nine stems**, and no anchor in this file is routed to it.
- ➡️ **Topic 03** owns liberalism as a doctrine, and ➡️ **Topic 05** owns Gandhi, socialism, fascism and anarchism as doctrines. This topic compares the **liberal view of politics** with its rivals without re-teaching liberalism itself.
- ➡️ **Topic 06** owns the sex/gender distinction and feminist theory. The husband-and-wife conversion case at Lesson 9 is a **logical** illustration of how a private issue becomes public, and carries no substantive gender claim.
- ➡️ **The caste owners** hold caste. This chapter's entire treatment is the parenthesis **(e.g. politicization of caste)**.
- ➡️ **Polity** owns constitutional articles, statutes, schemes, amendments and case law. Lesson 23's Indian application cites the **structure** of Fundamental Rights, Directive Principles and Fundamental Duties **without asserting article numbers**.
- ⚠️ **Within Political Theory**, this topic owns the question *what makes an activity political*, the chapter's five general characteristics, and the three rival views of politics with their scope conditions. Where a neighbouring topic owns a doctrine, this file teaches only the **view of politics** that doctrine generates.
